-module. Then, the complex
C^{\text{ad}}_{\bullet}(K^{S},M)
can be equipped with a natural right action of the subalgebra
\mathcal{H}(\Delta,K^{S})_{R}
of
\mathcal{H}(\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{F}^{S,\infty}),K^{S})_{R}
generated by the indicator functions
[K^{S}\delta K^{S}]
with
\delta\in\Delta
.
If
\delta_{1},...,\delta_{s}
is a system of representatives in the coset space
K^{S}\delta K^{S}/K^{S}
, which is finite, then this action is given by

(\sigma\otimes m)[K^{S}\delta K^{S}]=\sum_{j}\delta_{j}^{-1}\sigma\otimes\delta_{j}^{-1}m

for
\sigma\in C^{\text{sing}}_{\bullet}(X_{S}),m\in M
. Similarly, if
M
is a
R[G_{\infty}\times G_{S}\times\Delta]
-module, then
C_{\text{ad}}^{\bullet}(K^{S},M)
may be equipped with an action of
\mathcal{H}(\Delta,K^{S})_{R}
, where for
\delta
and
\delta_{1},...,\delta_{s}
as above,

([K^{S}\delta K^{S}]\phi)(\sigma)=\sum_{j}\delta_{j}\phi(\delta_{j}^{-1}\sigma))

for any
\phi\in C_{\text{ad}}^{\bullet}(K^{S},M)
and
\sigma\in C^{\text{sing}}_{\bullet}(X_{S})
.
Note that these actions come from particular cases of
Lemma

4.4
.
For any
\mathcal{K}^{S}
-modules
M
and
N
, there is a natural isomorphism

(5.1)

\displaystyle\text{Hom}_{R}(C^{\text{ad}}_{\bullet}(K^{S},M),N)\simeq C_{\text{ad}}^{\bullet}(K^{S},\text{Hom}_{R}(M,N))

which is equivariant for the action of
\mathcal{H}(\Delta,K^{S})_{R}
for all
v
.
Using the homotopy equivalences we have fixed at the end of
Section

5.1.3
, we can define actions of the elements of
\mathcal{H}(\Delta,K^{S})_{R}
on
C_{\bullet}(K^{S},M)
and
C^{\bullet}(K^{S},M)
in each respective case. Note, however, that the resulting actions of the different operators in
\mathcal{H}(\Delta,K^{S})_{R}
on these complexes only commute up to homotopy. In particular, this induces a well-defined action of
\mathcal{H}(\Delta,K^{S})_{R}
on (co)homology (or at the level of derived categories).
If the action of
\mathcal{K}^{S}
on
M
extends to an action of
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
, then we can always take
\Delta=G_{v}K^{S}
for any
v\not\in S_{\text{ram}}(K^{S})
, and we obtain actions of
\mathcal{H}(G_{v}K^{S},K^{S})_{R}\simeq\mathcal{H}(G_{v},K_{v})_{R}
on homology and cohomology. We set

\mathbb{T}^{S}(K^{S})_{R}=\bigotimes^{\prime}_{v\not\in S_{\text{ram}}(K^{S})}\mathcal{H}(G_{v},K_{v})_{R}

and
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K^{S})=\mathbb{T}^{S}(K^{S})_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
.

5.2.
Shapiro’s lemma

Next, we discuss a (Hecke-equivariant) version of Shapiro’s lemma for
S
-arithmetic homology, as well as a result on Hecke operators.
Both of these results admit analogues for cohomology, but we will work only with homology.
We continue to use the notation from the previous section, and let
T\subseteq S
be a subset and
K^{T}\subseteq\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{F}^{T,\infty})
an open compact subgroup of the form
K^{S}\times K_{S\smallsetminus T}
with
K_{S\smallsetminus T}\subseteq G_{S\smallsetminus T}
an open compact subgroup, and
\Delta_{S\smallsetminus T}\subseteq G_{S\smallsetminus T}
a submonoid containing
K_{S\smallsetminus T}
.
Set
\mathcal{K}^{T}:=G_{\infty}\times G_{T}\times K^{T}
.

By
[
BT72
, 3.3]
, there is a point
\alpha
in
\mathscr{B}_{S\smallsetminus T}
that is fixed by the action of
K_{S\smallsetminus T}
.
The projection map

X_{S}=\mathscr{B}_{\infty}\times\mathscr{B}_{S}\times\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{F})=\mathscr{B}_{\infty}\times\mathscr{B}_{T}\times\mathscr{B}_{S\smallsetminus T}\times\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{F})\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\mathscr{B}_{\infty}\times\mathscr{B}_{T}\times\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{F})=X_{T}

is a
(\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathcal{K}^{T})
-equivariant homotopy equivalence
X_{S}\simeq X_{T}
, whose inverse is given, up to
(\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathcal{K}^{T})
-equivariant homotopy, by the map

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{\infty}\times\mathscr{B}_{T}\times\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{F})

\displaystyle\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\mathscr{B}_{\infty}\times\mathscr{B}_{T}\times\mathscr{B}_{S\smallsetminus T}\times\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{F})

\displaystyle(b_{\infty},b_{T},h)

\displaystyle\longmapsto(b_{\infty},b_{T},h_{S\smallsetminus T}\alpha,h),

where
h_{S\smallsetminus T}
denotes the image of
h\in\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
in
G_{S\smallsetminus T}
.
Indeed, we have
f\circ g=\text{id}_{X_{T}}
, and

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{\infty}\times\mathscr{B}_{T}\times\mathscr{B}_{S\smallsetminus T}\times\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{F})\times[0,1]

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathscr{B}_{\infty}\times\mathscr{B}_{T}\times\mathscr{B}_{S\smallsetminus T}\times\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{F})

\displaystyle(b_{\infty},b_{T},\beta,h,t)

\displaystyle\longmapsto(b_{\infty},b_{T},(1-t)\beta+th_{S\smallsetminus T}\alpha,h)

is a
(\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathcal{K}^{T})
-equivariant homotopy between
\text{id}_{X_{S}}
and
g\circ f
.
Here, we have used the notation from
[
BT72
, Section 2.5]
.
Namely, there is an appartment in the building
\mathscr{B}_{S\smallsetminus T}
containing both
\beta
and
\alpha
, and
(1-t)\beta+th_{S\smallsetminus T}\alpha
runs through the line segment between these points for
t\in[0,1]
.

By
Lemma

2.29
, the association
M\longmapsto H_{0}(K^{T},M)=R[\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{F})]\otimes_{R[\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathcal{K}^{T}]}M
defines a right exact functor from the category of
R[\mathbf{G}(F)\times G_{\infty}\times G_{T}\times(\Delta_{S\smallsetminus T})^{-1}\times K^{S}]
to the category of right
\mathcal{H}(\Delta_{S\smallsetminus T},K_{S\smallsetminus T})_{R}
-modules.
The following proposition shows that we may see
T
-arithmetic homology, equipped with its Hecke action, as the left derived functors of degree 0 homology.

Proposition 5.2
.

The association
M_{\bullet}\longmapsto R[\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{F})]\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{R[\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathcal{K}^{T}]}M_{\bullet}
defines a functor from the derived category of
R[\mathbf{G}(F)\times G_{\infty}\times G_{T}\times(\Delta_{S\smallsetminus T})^{-1}\times K^{S}]
-modules to the derived category of right
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K^{S})\otimes\mathcal{H}(\Delta_{S\smallsetminus T},K_{S\smallsetminus T})_{R}
-modules such that the degree
n
homology of the complex
R[\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{F})]\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{R[\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathcal{K}^{T}]}M
is
H_{n}(K^{T},M)
with its natural right Hecke action.

Proof.
Consider
C^{\text{sing}}_{\bullet}(X_{S})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}R
, it is a free
R[\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathcal{K}^{S}]
-module resolution of
R[\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{F})]
, and hence also a free resolution of it as a module over
R[\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathcal{K}^{T}]
and over
R[\mathbf{G}(F)\times G_{\infty}\times G_{T}\times(\Delta_{S\smallsetminus T})^{-1}\times K^{S}]
.
The image of
M_{\bullet}
under functor above is thus computed by
\tot(C^{\text{sing}}_{\bullet}(X_{S})\otimes_{Z[\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathcal{K}^{T}]}M_{\bullet})
, where
\tot
denotes the total complex.
Now, for any such
M_{\bullet}
, the homotopy equivalences of complexes of
\mathbb{Z}[\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathcal{K}^{T}]
-modules

C_{\bullet}^{\text{sing}}(X_{S})\mathrel{{\mathop{\leftrightarrows}\limits}}C_{\bullet}^{\text{sing}}(X_{T}),

induced by the maps
f,g
above induces homotopy equivalences of
\mathbb{Z}[\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathcal{K}^{T}]
-modules

\tot(C_{\bullet}^{\text{sing}}(X_{S})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathcal{K}^{T}]}M_{\bullet})\mathrel{{\mathop{\leftrightarrows}\limits}}\tot(C_{\bullet}^{\text{sing}}(X_{T})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathcal{K}^{T}]}M_{\bullet}).

In order to prove the proposition, it’s enough to show that
f_{*}
is equivariant for the action of
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K^{S})
and the right action of
\mathcal{H}(\Delta_{S\smallsetminus T},K_{S\smallsetminus T})_{R}
on either side.
This follows immediately from the fact that
f_{*}\colon C_{\bullet}^{\text{sing}}(X_{S})\longrightarrow C_{\bullet}^{\text{sing}}(X_{T})
is in fact
\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
-equivariant (as
f
is).
∎

Next, we have the following version of Shapiro’s lemma.

Proposition 5.3
.

Assume for simplicity that
\mathcal{H}(\Delta_{S\smallsetminus T},K_{S\smallsetminus T})_{R}
is commutative.
Let
M
be a
R[\mathbf{G}(F)\times G_{\infty}\times G_{T}\times(\Delta_{S\smallsetminus T})^{-1}\times\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{F}^{S,\infty})]
-module, which we view as a module over
R[\mathbf{G}(F)\times G_{\infty}\times G_{T}\times(\Delta_{S\smallsetminus T})^{-1}\times K^{S}]
by restriction. Consider the compact induction
\cInd_{\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathcal{K}^{T}}^{\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathcal{K}^{S}}(M)\simeq\cInd_{K_{S\smallsetminus T}}^{G_{S\smallsetminus T}}(M)
with its action of
\mathcal{H}(\Delta_{S\smallsetminus T},K_{S\smallsetminus T})_{R}
as in
Lemma

4.4
.
There is a functorial isomorphism in
D(\mathbb{T}^{S}(K^{S})_{R}\otimes\mathcal{H}(\Delta_{S\smallsetminus T},K_{S\smallsetminus T})_{R})

(5.4)

\displaystyle C_{\bullet}(K^{S},\cInd_{\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathcal{K}^{T}}^{\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathcal{K}^{S}}(M))\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}C_{\bullet}(K^{S},M).

Proof.
By
Proposition

5.2
, it’s enough to prove the statement with
Equation

5.4
replaced by

(5.5)

\displaystyle R[\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{F})]\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{R[\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathcal{K}^{S}]}\cInd_{\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathcal{K}^{T}}^{\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathcal{K}^{S}}(M)\simeq R[\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{F})]\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{R[\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathcal{K}^{T}]}M

There is an isomorphism
\cInd_{\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathcal{K}^{T}}^{\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathcal{K}^{S}}(M)\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}R[\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathcal{K}^{S}]\otimes_{R[\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathcal{K}^{T}]}M
sending
[g,m]
to
g\otimes m
. Moreover, the image of the target in
D(\mathcal{H}(\Delta_{S\smallsetminus T},K_{S\smallsetminus T})_{R})
is isomorphic to

R[\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathcal{K}^{S}]\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{R[\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathcal{K}^{T}]}M
since
R[\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathcal{K}^{S}]
is free over
R[\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathcal{K}^{T}]
.
Thus, we see that the isomorphism
Equation

5.5
holds in
D(R)
, so it’s enough to show that for any module
Q
over
R[\mathbf{G}(F)\times G_{\infty}\times G_{T}\times(\Delta_{S\smallsetminus T})^{-1}\times\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{F}^{S,\infty})]
, the isomorphism

\phi\colon Q\otimes_{R[\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathcal{K}^{S}]}\cInd_{\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathcal{K}^{T}}^{\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathcal{K}^{S}}(M)\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}Q\otimes_{R[\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathcal{K}^{T}]}M

is equivariant for the actions of
\mathcal{H}(\Delta_{S\smallsetminus T},K_{S\smallsetminus T})_{R}
and
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K^{S})_{R}
. The case of
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K^{S})_{R}
is straightforward, so let us show this only for
\mathcal{H}(\Delta_{S\smallsetminus T},K_{S\smallsetminus T})_{R}
.
If
\delta\in\Delta_{S\smallsetminus T}
and we write
K_{S\smallsetminus T}\delta K_{S\smallsetminus\Delta}=\coprod_{j}\delta_{j}K_{S\smallsetminus T}
, we have

\displaystyle q\otimes[g,m]

\displaystyle\xmapsto{[K_{S\smallsetminus T}\delta K_{S\smallsetminus T}]}q\otimes([g,m][K_{S\smallsetminus T}\delta K_{S\smallsetminus T}])=q\otimes\sum_{j}[g\delta_{j},\delta_{j}^{-1}m]

\displaystyle\mathrel{{\mathop{\longmapsto}\limits}}\sum_{j}\delta_{j}^{-1}g^{-1}q\otimes\delta_{j}^{-1}m,

whereas

\displaystyle q\otimes[g,m]

\displaystyle\mathrel{{\mathop{\longmapsto}\limits}}g^{-1}q\otimes m

\displaystyle\xmapsto{[K_{S\smallsetminus T}\delta K_{S\smallsetminus T}]}\sum_{j}\delta_{j}^{-1}g^{-1}q\otimes\delta_{j}^{-1}m=\sum_{j}\delta_{j}^{-1}g^{-1}q\otimes\delta_{j}^{-1}m.\qed

5.3.
The global setting and local data

Let us now introduce the global setting that we will place ourselves in the rest of the article.
Let
F
be a number field and
\mathbf{G}
a reductive group over
F
.
Write
\mathcal{S}_{p}
for the set of primes of
F
above
p
, and let
R
be an affinoid
{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
-algebra.

Let
S
be a finite set of non-archimedean primes of
F
.
For each
v\in S
we let
\mathbf{G}_{v}=\mathbf{G}_{F_{v}}
and place ourselves in the setting of
Section

2.9
and
Section

4
– we will use the notation therein.
Thus, for example, we have fixed for all
v
a maximal
F_{v}
-split torus
\mathbf{S}_{v}
contained in a maximal torus
\mathbf{T}_{v}
defined over
F_{v}
, an ordering of the relative root system
\Phi_{v}
of
(\mathbf{G}_{v},\mathbf{S}_{v})
, and a parabolic subgroup
\mathbf{P}_{v}
with Levi subgroup
\mathbf{M}_{v}
containing
\mathbf{T}_{v}
, etc.
We set
G_{v}=\mathbf{G}_{v}(F_{v})
and
G=G_{S}=\prod_{v\in S}G_{v}
and similarly for the other subgroups of
G_{v}
, and analogously
\Phi=\coprod_{v\in S}\Phi_{v}
, etc.
We will never use
S
to denote the points of the maximal split torus, so there should be no confusion with our set of primes
S
.

If
\Sigma
is a subset of
\mathcal{S}_{p}
, then we will write
\mathbf{G}_{\Sigma}=\prod_{v\in\Sigma}\Res_{F_{v}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathbf{G}_{v}
,
G_{\Sigma}=\mathbf{G}_{\Sigma}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
\mathbf{G}_{\Sigma,E}=E\times_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathbf{G}_{\Sigma}
for any finite extension
E/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, and similarly for
\mathbf{M}_{\Sigma}
, etc. We will also fix for
v\in\mathcal{S}_{p}\smallsetminus S
a maximal torus
\mathbf{T}_{v}\subseteq\mathbf{G}_{v}
defined over
F_{v}
. For any
v\in\mathcal{S}_{p}
, we let
L^{\prime}_{v}
be a finite Galois extension of
F_{v}
over which
\mathbf{T}_{v}
splits.
If
E
is a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
in
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
containing the images of all the
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-algebra embeddings of the
L^{\prime}_{v}
into
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
, then
\mathbf{G}_{\mathcal{S}_{p},E}=\prod_{v\in\mathcal{S}_{p}}\prod_{\tau\colon F_{v}\xhookrightarrow{}E}E\times_{F_{v},\tau}\mathbf{G}_{v}
is split with maximal split torus
\mathbf{T}_{\mathcal{S}_{p},E}
.
Fix for each
v\in\mathcal{S}_{p}
and embedding
\tau\colon F_{v}\xhookrightarrow{}E
, fix a Borel subgroup
\mathbf{B}_{v,\tau}
of
E\times_{F_{v},\tau}\mathbf{G}_{v}
containing
E\times_{F_{v},\tau}\mathbf{T}_{v}
and contained in
E\times_{F_{v},\tau}\mathbf{P}_{v}
whenever
v\in S
, and write
\mathbf{B}_{\mathcal{S}_{p},E}=\prod_{v,\tau}\mathbf{B}_{v,\tau}
.

Let us now fix
\Sigma\subseteq S_{p}:=S\cap\mathcal{S}_{p}
.
We will use the results from
Section

4.3
applied to the decomposition
G=G_{\Sigma}\times G^{\Sigma}
with

\displaystyle G_{\Sigma}

\displaystyle=\prod_{v\in\Sigma}G_{v}=\mathbf{G}_{\Sigma}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}),

\displaystyle G^{\Sigma}

\displaystyle=\prod_{v\in S\smallsetminus\Sigma}G_{v}.

Starting in
Section

5.5
, we will let
\sigma
be a representation of a subgroup
\mathpzc{M}
of
\mathfrak{M}
containing
AM_{0}
on a finitely generated
R
-module as in
Section

4.2
.
We will assume it is of the form
\sigma=\bigboxtimes_{v\in\Sigma}\sigma_{v}
, where
\sigma_{v}
is a locally analytic (resp. smooth) representation of
G_{v}
if
v\in\Sigma
(resp.
v\in S\smallsetminus\Sigma
), although many of our results hold without making this assumption.
Set also
\sigma_{\Sigma}:=\bigboxtimes_{v\in\Sigma}\sigma_{v}
and
\sigma^{\Sigma}:=\bigboxtimes_{v\in S\smallsetminus\Sigma}\sigma_{v}
, and
V:=\cInd_{\mathpzc{M}}^{M}(\sigma)
, which by
Lemma

4.2
is isomorphic to
V_{\Sigma}\widehat{\otimes}_{E}V^{\Sigma}
for
V_{\Sigma}:=\cInd_{\mathpzc{M}_{\Sigma}}^{M_{\Sigma}}(\sigma_{\Sigma})
and
V^{\Sigma}:=\cInd_{\mathpzc{M}^{\Sigma}}^{M^{\Sigma}}(\sigma^{\Sigma})
(and similarly for other subsets of
S
).
Let
U
be a representation of
G_{\mathcal{S}_{p}}
on a finite flat
R
-module.
We will view it as a representation of
G_{\mathcal{S}_{p}}\times G
by letting
G
act trivially.
In
Section

5.4
our notation will be different and we will describe it therein.
Instead of
\mathcal{H}(J\mathpzc{M}^{-}J,J)_{R}
we will write
\mathcal{H}_{S,R}^{-}
, or
\mathcal{H}_{S,\mathpzc{M},R}^{-}
when we want to make the subgroup
\mathpzc{M}
explicit.
Define for
s
sufficiently large the
J\mathpzc{M}^{+}J
-modules
\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{s-{\Sigma-\text{an}}}
as in
Section

4.2
and write
\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,U}^{s-{\Sigma-\text{an}}}
for their continuous
R
-duals.
Define also
s_{\sigma}
as in
Section

4.2
.
To simplify notation, we will write
\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{{\Sigma-\text{la}}}
and
\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,U}^{{\Sigma-\text{la}}}
for
U\otimes_{R}\Ind_{J\cap P}^{J}(\sigma)^{{\Sigma-\text{la}}}
and its continuous
R
-linear dual, and
\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V)
for
U\otimes_{R}\Ind_{P}^{G}(V)^{{\Sigma-\text{la}}}
.
Sometimes we don’t want to distinguish between
\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{s-{\Sigma-\text{an}}}
for
s\geq s_{\sigma}
and
\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{{\Sigma-\text{la}}}
, so we will write
\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma}
for either of these, and similarly for
\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma}
.
We will view these as representations of
G_{S\cup\mathcal{S}_{p}}
via the embedding
G_{S\cup\mathcal{S}_{p}}\xhookrightarrow{}G_{\mathcal{S}_{p}}\times G
.

Fix an isomorphism
\iota\colon\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\mathbb{C}
. Let
E
be a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
contained in
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
, and assume that
R
is an affinoid
E
-algebra.
Our main case of interest is the case when
U
is an (irreducible) algebraic representation of
G_{\mathcal{S}_{p}\smallsetminus\Sigma}
(see
Section

5.7
for what we mean by this) and
\sigma
is the twist by a locally analytic character of
M
or of
\mathpzc{M}
of the tensor product of an (irreducible) algebraic representation of
M_{\Sigma}
and a smooth representation
\sigma_{0}
of
M
over
E
such that
\mathbb{C}\otimes_{E,\iota}\cInd_{\mathpzc{M}}^{M}(\sigma_{0})
is a supercuspidal representation of
M
.
As we mentioned in the introduction, it follows from
[
BK93
]
,
[
Ste08
]
,
[
SS08
]
,
[
Fin21
]
that in many cases, all supercuspidal representations of
M
can be obtained in this manner.
For example, from the latter we can deduce the following result.

Theorem 5.6
.

Assume that for all
v\in S
,
\mathbf{M}_{v}
splits over a tamely ramified extension of
F_{v}
and
v
does not divide the order of the Weyl group of
\overline{F}_{v}\times_{F_{v}}\mathbf{M}_{v}
. Let
\pi
be a supercuspidal representation of
M
. Then, there exists a facet
\mathscr{F}_{M}
in the Bruhat–Tits building of
\mathbf{M}
, a subgroup
\mathpzc{M}
of
\mathfrak{M}
containing
AM_{0}
(where
\mathfrak{M}
and
M_{0}
are defined in terms of the facet
\mathscr{F}_{M}
as in
Section

2
), a finite extension
E/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
in
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
and an irreducible smooth representation
\sigma_{0}
over
E
admitting a central character such that
\pi\simeq\mathbb{C}\otimes_{E,\iota}\cInd_{\mathpzc{M}}^{M}(\sigma_{0})
.

Proof.
This follows from the main result of
[
Fin21
]
.
The representation
\pi
decomposes as a tensor product
\pi=\bigotimes_{v\in S}\pi_{v}
of supercuspidal representations of
M_{v}
for
v\in S
.
According to
[
Fin21
, Theorem 8.1]
, there exists for each
v
a facet
\mathscr{F}_{M_{v}}
of the Bruhat–Tits building of
\mathbf{M}_{v}
giving rise to subgroups
M_{0},\mathfrak{M}
, and a representation
\sigma_{v,\mathbb{C}}
of a finite index subgroup
\mathpzc{M}_{v}
of
\mathfrak{M}_{v}
containing
A_{v}
such that
\pi\simeq\cInd_{\mathpzc{M}_{v}}^{M_{v}}(\sigma_{v,\mathbb{C}})
.
By compactly inducing
\sigma_{v,\mathbb{C}}
if necessary, we may assume that
\mathpzc{M}_{v}
contains
A_{v}M_{v,0}
.
Extend scalars along
\iota^{-1}
to obtain a representation
\sigma_{v,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
over
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.
Since
\mathpzc{M}_{v}
is topologically finitely generated for all
v\in S
, there exists an extension
E
of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
in
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
and a representations
\sigma_{v}
of
\mathpzc{M}_{v}
over
E
such that
\sigma_{v,\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}=\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\otimes_{E_{v}}\sigma_{v}
for all
v
.
Finally, take
\sigma_{0}=\bigotimes_{v\in S}\sigma_{v}
and
\mathpzc{M}=\prod_{v\in S}\mathpzc{M}_{v}
.
∎

We will consider
\spadesuit
-arithmetic homology and cohomology for two different
\spadesuit
:

•

The arithmetic case,
\spadesuit=\emptyset
. We will usually omit the subindex
S
from the notation in the previous section. Fix a compact open subgroup
K^{S}
of
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{F}^{S,\infty})
. Our compact open subgroups will usually be
J\times K^{S}
we will consider Hecke operators with respect to the monoids
\Delta_{S}=J\mathpzc{M}^{-}J\times K^{S}
and
\Delta_{v}=G_{v}K^{S}
for
v\not\in S_{\text{ram}}(K^{S})
. We fix a Borel–Serre complex and homotopy equivalences as in
Section

5.1.3
.

•

The
S
-arithmetic case,
\spadesuit=S
. We have already fixed a compact open subgroup
K^{S}
of
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{F}^{S,\infty})
. Let us also fix Borel–Serre complexes and homotopy equivalences as in
Section

5.1.3
. When defining Hecke operators we take
\Delta_{v}=G_{v}K^{S}
for any
v\not\in S_{\text{ram}}(K^{S})
.

Finally, we will also write
\mathbb{T}(K^{S}):=\mathbb{T}^{S}(K^{S})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathcal{H}_{S,{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}^{-}
.

5.4.
Overconvergent homology

Next, we will recall the definition and state some of the properties of (parabolic) overconvergent homology (see
[
AS08
]
,
[
Han17
]
,
[
Urb11
]
,
[
SW21
]
) adapted to our general context, where we don’t assume that
\mathbb{Q}_{p}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\Res_{F/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbf{G}
is quasi-split.

We will assume in this section that
\sigma
is a locally analytic representation of
M_{0}
(instead of the bigger group
\mathpzc{M}
) on a finite free
R
-module, of the form
\sigma=\sigma_{\Sigma}\otimes\sigma^{\Sigma}
with
\sigma_{\Sigma}
a locally analytic representation of
M_{\Sigma,0}
and
\sigma^{\Sigma}
a smooth representation of
M_{0}^{\Sigma}
. We may define
R
-modules
\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{s-{\Sigma-\text{an}}},\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{{\Sigma-\text{la}}},\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma},\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,U}^{s-{\Sigma-\text{an}}},\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,U}^{{\Sigma-\text{la}}},\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma}
as in
Section

5.3
equipped with an action of
J
for
s\geq s_{\sigma}
for some
s_{\sigma}\geq 0
. However, since
\sigma
is not equipped with an action of
AM_{0}
, this action does not extend to an action of
JA^{+}J
(or
JA^{-}J
for the duals) as in
Lemma

4.10
(and thus to any monoid of the form
J\mathpzc{M}^{+}J
where
\mathpzc{M}
contains
AM_{0}
).
Instead, we note that we are in a similar situation to that of
Section

4.4
: the inclusion
\Lambda\xhookrightarrow{}JAJ
induces a bijection between
\Lambda
and
J\backslash JAJ/J
. If we denote its inverse by
\eta
, then the action of
J
on
\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{s-{\Sigma-\text{an}}}
can be extended to an action of
JA^{+}J
by the formula

(a*F)(\overline{n})=F(a^{-1}\overline{n}\eta(a)).

As remarked in
Section

4.4
, if the action of
\sigma
does extend to an action of
AM_{0}
and
\Lambda
acts via a character
\chi
, then this agrees with the
*
-action we have previously defined.
For this reason, we will denote these spaces by
\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U,*}^{s-{\Sigma-\text{an}}},\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,U,*}^{s-{\Sigma-\text{an}}}
, etc.

The
R
-module
\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U,*}^{s-{\Sigma-\text{an}}}
is an orthonormalisable Banach
R
-module, and for any
a\in\Lambda^{-}
(resp.
a\in\Lambda_{\Sigma}^{--}
) the action of
a^{-1}
gives a norm-decreasing (resp.
R
-compact) homomorphism
\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U,*}^{s-{\Sigma-\text{an}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U,*}^{s-{\Sigma-\text{an}}}
(by the arguments in the paragraph above
Lemma

4.11
).
As we described in
Section

5.1
, the action of the monoid
JA^{+}J
induces an action of
\mathcal{H}_{S,R}^{-}
on
C_{\bullet}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U,*}^{s-{\Sigma-\text{an}}})
. We denote the action of
[JaJ]
by
U_{a}
as usual. This complex of
R
-modules is a finite direct sum of copies of
\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U,*}^{s-{\Sigma-\text{an}}}
, and we see from the corresponding statement for the
*
-action that the operator
U_{a}
on this complex is norm-decreasing (resp.
R
-compact) when this space is given its direct sum Banach topology.

We can use this to obtain properties of slope decompositions of
C_{\bullet}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U,*}^{s-{\Sigma-\text{an}}})
with respect to
U_{a}
. Let us recall what this means.
If
Q(X)\in R[X]
is a polynomial of degree
d
, we write
Q^{*}(X)=X^{d}Q(1/X)
.
Given a point
x
of
\text{Sp}(R)
, write
Q_{x}(X)
for the specialisation of
Q(X)
at
x
.
Let
h\in\mathbb{Q}_{\geq 0}
.
We say that
Q(X)
has slope-
\leq h
if for every point
x
of
\text{Sp}(R)
, every edge in the Newton polygon of
Q_{x}(X)
has slope
\leq h
, and that
Q(X)
is multiplicative if its leading coefficient is a multiplicative unit (for a fixed norm of
R
), i.e. it is a unit
x\in R^{\times}
satisfying
|x^{-1}|=|x|^{-1}
. If
W
is an
R
-module equipped with an
R
-linear operator
U
, then we say that a vector
w\in W
has slope
\leq h
if there exists some polynomial
Q(X)\in R[X]
of slope
\leq h
such that
Q^{*}(U)w=0
. Finally, we say that a
U
-stable decomposition
W=W_{\leq h}\oplus W_{>h}
is a slope-
\leq h
decomposition of
W
(with respect to
U
) if
W_{\leq h}
is finitely generated and consists of elements of slope
\leq h
and
Q^{*}(U)\colon W_{>h}\longrightarrow W_{>h}
is an
E
-linear isomorphism for every multiplicative polynomial
Q(X)
of slope
\leq h
. If such a decomposition exists, then it is unique. We refer to
[
Han17
, Section 2.3]
for a summary of the basic properties of slope decompositions. Let us fix some
a\in A^{-}
such that
a^{-1}*\mathcal{A}_{\sigma}^{s-\text{an}}\subseteq\mathcal{A}_{\sigma}^{(s-1)-\text{an}}
; all slope decompositions will be taken with respect to the operator
U_{a}
.

Proposition 5.7
.

Assume that
C_{\bullet}(K^{p}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U,*}^{s-{\Sigma-\text{an}}})
admits a slope-
\leq h
decomposition

C_{\bullet}(K^{p}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U,*}^{s-{\Sigma-\text{an}}})=C_{\bullet}(K^{p}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U,*}^{s-{\Sigma-\text{an}}})_{\leq h}\oplus C_{\bullet}(K^{p}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U,*}^{s-{\Sigma-\text{an}}})_{>h}.

Then:

(i)

For any
s^{\prime}\geq s_{\sigma}
, the complexes
C_{\bullet}(K^{p}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U,*}^{s^{\prime}-{\Sigma-\text{an}}})
and
C_{\bullet}(K^{p}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U,*}^{{\Sigma-\text{la}}})
admit slope-
\leq h
decompositions with

\displaystyle C_{\bullet}(K^{p}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U,*}^{s-{\Sigma-\text{an}}})_{\leq h}

\displaystyle\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}C_{\bullet}(K^{p}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U,*}^{s^{\prime}-{\Sigma-\text{an}}})_{\leq h}

\displaystyle\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}C_{\bullet}(K^{p}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U,*}^{{\Sigma-\text{la}}})_{\leq h}.

(ii)

H_{*}(K^{p}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U,*}^{\Sigma})
admits a slope-
\leq h
decomposition whose slope-
\leq h
part is the homology of
C_{\bullet}(K^{p}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U,*}^{\Sigma})_{\leq h}
.

(iii)

For any Banach
R
-algebra
R^{\prime}
,

C_{\bullet}(K^{p}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma\otimes_{R}R^{\prime},U\otimes_{R}R^{\prime},*}^{\Sigma})_{\leq h}\simeq C_{\bullet}(K^{p}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U,*}^{\Sigma})_{\leq h}\otimes_{R}R^{\prime}.

(iv)

For any Banach
R
-algebra
R^{\prime}
, there is a first-quadrant homological spectral sequence

E^{2}_{i,j}=\text{Tor}_{i}^{R}(H_{j}(K^{p}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U,*}^{\Sigma})_{\leq h},R^{\prime})\implies H_{i+j}(K^{p}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma\otimes_{R}R^{\prime},U\otimes_{R}R^{\prime},*}^{\Sigma})_{\leq h}

In particular, if
R^{\prime}
is flat over
R
, then

H_{*}(K^{p}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma\otimes_{R}R^{\prime},U\otimes_{R}R^{\prime},*}^{\Sigma})_{\leq h}\simeq H_{*}(K^{p}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U,*}^{\Sigma})_{\leq h}\otimes_{R}R^{\prime}.

(v)

For a flat Banach
R
-algebra
R^{\prime}
, let

F_{R^{\prime}}^{s}(X)=\det(1-XU_{a}|C_{\bullet}(K^{p}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma\otimes_{R}R^{\prime},U\otimes_{R}R^{\prime},*}^{s-{\Sigma-\text{an}}}))\in R^{\prime}\{\!\{X\}\!\}

be the characteristic power series of the operator
U_{a}
on
C_{\bullet}(K^{p}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma\otimes_{R}R^{\prime},U\otimes_{R}R^{\prime},*}^{s-{\Sigma-\text{an}}})
. Then,
F_{R}^{s}(X)
is independent of
s\geq s_{\sigma}
and
F_{R^{\prime}}^{s}(X)
is the image of
F_{R}^{s}(X)
in
R^{\prime}\{\!\{X\}\!\}
.

Proof.
Everything follows from the same arguments as in
[
Han17
, Sections 3.1 and 3.3]
.
∎

5.5.
S
-arithmetic homology of parabolic induction

Or next goal is to relate the
S
-arithmetic homology of
\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V)
to the arithmetic homology of
\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma}
via the quasi-isomorphism from
Theorem

4.19
.
Recall that we view
\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V)
as a module over
\mathcal{H}_{S,R}^{-}
by letting
U_{\mu}
act as the identity for all
\mu
.
We will do the same for the Borel–Serre complex
C_{\bullet}(K^{S},\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V))
.

Proposition 5.8
.

There is an isomorphism in
D(\mathbb{T}(K^{S})_{R})
2
2

2

Similar to our remark at the beginning of
Section

4.3
, it would be more precise to write
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K^{S})_{R}(1)\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathbb{T}(K^{S})_{R}}{-}
instead of
R(1)\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{H}_{S,R}^{-}}{-}
.

C_{\bullet}(K^{S},\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V))\simeq R(1)\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{H}_{S,R}^{-}}C_{\bullet}(K^{S},\cInd_{J}^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma})).

Proof.
Let
C_{\bullet}
be a projective
R[\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{F})]
-module resolution of
R[\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{F})]
and
P_{\bullet}
be a projective
\mathcal{H}_{S,R}^{-}
-module resolution of
R(1)
.
Consider the map
\varphi
from
Section

4.3
,

P_{\bullet}\otimes_{\mathcal{H}_{S,R}^{-}}\cInd_{J}^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma})\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V),

which is a quasi-isomorphism and commutes with the action of
G
.
In particular, we see that there are quasi-isomorphisms

\displaystyle R(1)\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{H}_{S,R}^{-}}C_{\bullet}(K^{S},\cInd_{J}^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma}))

\displaystyle\simeq\tot\left(P_{\bullet}\otimes_{\mathcal{H}_{S,R}^{-}}(\cInd_{J}^{G}(\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma})\otimes_{R[\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathcal{K}^{S}]}C_{\bullet})\right)

\displaystyle\simeq\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V)\otimes_{R[\mathbf{G}(F)\times\mathcal{K}^{S}]}C_{\bullet}

\displaystyle\simeq C_{\bullet}(K^{S},\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V))

The composition of these quasi-isomorphisms is
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K^{S})
-equivariant, since it is the homomorphism induced by the
G
-equivariant map
\varphi
.
∎

Combining this with Shapiro’s lemma,
Proposition

5.3
, we obtain the following.

Corollary 5.9
.

There is an isomorphism in
D(\mathbb{T}(K^{S})_{R})

C_{\bullet}(K^{S},\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V))\simeq R(1)\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{H}_{S,R}^{-}}C_{\bullet}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma}).

In particular, there is a
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K^{S})
-equivariant first quadrant (homological) spectral sequence of
R
-modules

E^{2}_{i,j}=\text{Tor}^{\mathcal{H}_{S,R}^{-}}_{i}(R(1),H_{j}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma}))\implies H_{i+j}(K^{S},\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V)).

In many cases it is convenient to replace the space
H_{*}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma})
with something smaller.
In this situation, the following lemma will be useful.
It can be applied, for example, to slope-
\leq h
decompositions when
|\chi(\mu)^{-1}|\leq p^{h}
.

Lemma 5.10
.

Recall that
\mathcal{H}^{-}_{S,R}\simeq R[\mathpzc{M}/M_{0}]
.
Let
\chi\colon\mathpzc{M}/M_{0}\longrightarrow R^{\times}
be a character and let
M
be an
\mathcal{H}_{S,R}^{-}
-module admitting a decomposition
M=M_{1}\oplus M_{2}
such that, for some
\mu\in\mathpzc{M}^{-}
the operator
1-\chi(\mu)^{-1}U_{\mu}
acts invertibly on
M_{2}
. Then,

R(\chi)\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{H}_{S,R}^{-}}M\simeq R(\chi)\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{H}_{S,R}^{-}}M_{1}.

Proof.
It’s enough to show that
R(1)\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{H}_{S,R}^{-}}M_{2}=0
. This follows from the fact that
1-\chi(\mu)^{-1}U_{\mu}
is zero on
R(\chi)
but is invertible on
M_{2}
.
∎

Applying this to the character
U_{\mu}\longmapsto 1
, it follows from this that if
C_{\bullet}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma})
admits a slope-
\leq 0
decomposition with respect to some
U_{\mu}
, then
H_{*}(K^{S},\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V))
is finitely generated over
R
. We will see in
Section

6.3
that this is true even when there is no slope-
\leq 0
decomposition. Let us remark that if
\mu\in\mathpzc{M}^{-}
(resp.
a\in A_{\Sigma}^{--}
) then the operator
U_{\mu}
(resp.
U_{a}
) on
C_{\bullet}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{s-{\Sigma-\text{an}}})
is continuous (resp.
R
-compact).

Lemma 5.11
.

Assume that we are in the situation from
Section

4.4
. There is an isomorphism in
D(\mathbb{T}(K^{S})_{R})

R(\chi)\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{H}_{S,R}^{-}}C_{\bullet}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U,*}^{\Sigma})\simeq R(\chi)\otimes_{R}\left(R(1)\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{H}_{S,R}^{-}}C_{\bullet}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma})\right).

Proof.
This follows from the fact that the functor
R(\chi)\otimes_{R}{-}
on
\mathcal{H}_{S,R}^{-}
-modules is exact, preserves projectives, and
R(\chi)\otimes_{R}(M\otimes_{\mathcal{H}_{S,R}^{-}}N)\simeq(R(\chi)\otimes_{R}M)\otimes_{\mathcal{H}_{S,R}^{-}}(R(\chi)\otimes_{R}N)
for any
\mathcal{H}_{S,R}^{-}
-modules
M,N
.
∎

5.6.
Finiteness and vanishing over a point

In the case where
R
is a finite field extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, we can say more about the
S
-arithmetic homology spaces from the previous section.
Throughout this section, we assume that
R
is such an extension, and write
E
instead of
R
to denote this.
Write
\mathfrak{m}_{1}
for the ideal of
\mathcal{H}_{S,E}^{-}
generated by
U_{\mu}-1
for all
\mu\in\mathpzc{M}^{-}
.
Given an
\mathcal{H}_{S,E}^{-}
-module
\mathcal{M}
and
n\geq 0
, we write
\mathcal{M}[\mathfrak{m}_{1}^{n}]
for the submodule of elements which are killed by the ideal
\mathfrak{m}_{1}^{n}
and
\mathcal{M}[\mathfrak{m}_{1}^{\infty}]=\bigcup_{n\geq 0}\mathcal{M}[\mathfrak{m}_{1}^{n}]
. This is the generalised eigenspace for the system of eigenvalues
U_{\mu}\longmapsto 1
.
As in
Section

5.4
, when
a\in A_{\Sigma}^{--}
the operator
U_{a}
is compact.

Proposition 5.12
.

There is an isomorphism in
D(\mathbb{T}(K^{S})_{E})

E(1)\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{H}_{S,E}^{-}}C_{\bullet}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma})\simeq E(1)\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{H}_{S,E}^{-}}C_{\bullet}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma})[\mathfrak{m}_{1}^{\infty}].

There is a
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K^{S})
-equivariant spectral sequence

E^{2}_{i,j}=\text{Tor}^{\mathcal{H}_{S,E}^{-}}_{i}(E(1),H_{j}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma})[\mathfrak{m}_{1}^{\infty}])\implies H_{i+j}(K^{S},\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V)).

Proof.
By
[
Urb11
, Theorem 2.3.8]
and the compactness of the operators
U_{a}
for
a\in A_{\Sigma}^{--}
, the complex of Banach spaces
C_{\bullet}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{s-{\Sigma-\text{an}}})
admits a slope-
\leq 0
decomposition with respect to some
U_{a}
. As in
Proposition

5.7
, this implies that
C_{\bullet}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{{\Sigma-\text{la}}})
also has a slope-
\leq 0
decomposition. Applying
Lemma

5.10
to these decompositions, we see that

E(1)\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{H}_{S,E}^{-}}C_{\bullet}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma})\simeq E(1)\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathcal{H}_{S,E}^{-}}C_{\bullet}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma})_{\leq 0}

As
C_{\bullet}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma})_{\leq 0}
is finite-dimensional (in each degree), there is a direct sum decomposition

C_{\bullet}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma})_{\leq 0}=C_{\bullet}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma})[\mathfrak{m}_{1}^{\infty}]\oplus C_{\bullet}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma})_{\leq 0}^{\mathcal{H}_{S,E}^{-}\neq 1}

where, for some
\mu\in\mathpzc{M}^{-}
,
U_{\mu}-1
acts invertibly on the second summand. Applying
Lemma

5.10
to this decomposition the result follows.
The existence of the spectral sequence then follows as usual.
∎

Corollary 5.13
.

(i)The vector space
H_{*}(K^{S},\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V))
is finite-dimensional.

(ii)

H_{*}(K^{S},\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V))
vanishes unless
1
is an eigenvalue of
U_{\mu}
in
H_{*}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma})
for all
\mu\in\mathpzc{M}^{-}
.

(iii)

Assume that for some choice of norm on the Banach space
U\otimes_{E}\sigma
, some element
\mu
of
\mathpzc{M}^{+}
acts as an operator of norm
<1
. Then
H_{*}(K^{S},\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V))=0
.

Proof.
As
H_{*}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma})[\mathfrak{m}_{1}^{\infty}]
is finite-dimensional, we see by using a resolution of
E(1)
by finite free
\mathcal{H}_{S,E}^{-}
-modules to compute the Tor groups in the
E^{2}
page of the spectral sequence from
Proposition

5.12
that these are again finite-dimensional. This proves (i).
If for some
\mu\in\mathpzc{M}^{-}
,
1
is not an eigenvalue of
U_{\mu}
in
H_{*}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma})
, then the generalised eigenspace for 1 of
H_{*}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma})
is zero, so all the terms in the
E^{2}
page of this spectral sequence vanish, and hence so does
H_{*}(K^{S},\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V))
.
Finally, if
\mu
is as in (iii) and
x
is its norm as an operator on
U\otimes_{E}\sigma
for some choice of norm on this space, then the action
\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{s-{\Sigma-\text{an}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{s-{\Sigma-\text{an}}}
of
\mu
has norm
\leq x
(for the norm induced by that of
U\otimes_{E}\sigma
via the obvious analogue of
Equation

4.9
including
U
), and one can deduce from this
that the operator
U_{\mu^{-1}}
on
C_{\bullet}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{s-{\Sigma-\text{an}}})
also has norm
\leq x
. The same must then be true of its eigenvalues, so by (ii), if
S
-arithmetic homology is non-zero then
1\leq x
.
∎

We will now shift our focus from homology to cohomology. A lot of the previous statements have analogues in this setting. Recall that we write
({-})^{\prime}
to denote the continuous duals of locally convex vector spaces over
E
.

Proposition 5.14
.

The locally convex topology on
H_{*}(K^{S},\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V))
induced by that of
C_{\bullet}(K^{S},\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V))
is the Hausdorff topology.
There is a
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K^{S})
-equivariant isomorphism

H_{n}(K^{S},\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V))^{\prime}\simeq H^{n}(K^{S},\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V)^{\prime})

for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
. Moreover, there is a first-quadrant
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K^{S})
-equivariant (cohomological) spectral sequence

E_{2}^{i,j}=\text{Ext}_{\mathcal{H}_{S,E}^{-}}^{i}(E(1),H^{j}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma})[\mathfrak{m}_{1}^{\infty}])\implies H^{i+j}(K^{S},\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V)^{\prime}).

Proof.
For each
n
,
C_{n}(K^{S},\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V))
is naturally a locally convex vector space of compact type, as it is a direct sum of finitely many copies of
\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V)
, which is itself of compact type. Write
Z_{n}\subseteq C_{n}(K^{S},\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V))
for the subspace of
n
-cycles, and choose a finite-dimensional subspace
W_{n}
of
Z_{n}
splitting the projection map
Z_{n}\longrightarrow H_{n}(K^{S},\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V))
. The subspace
W_{n}
is then Banach, as it is finite-dimensional and Hausdorff, and the
E
-linear map

C_{n+1}(K^{S},\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V))/Z_{n+1}\oplus W_{n}\longrightarrow Z_{n}

is a continuous and bijective map of compact type spaces, and hence an isomorphism by the open mapping theorem
[
Eme17
, Theorem 1.1.17]
.

In particular,
W_{n}
is topologically isomorphic to
H_{n}(K^{S},\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V))
, which proves the first statement.

A posteriori
, all the differentials in the complex

C_{\bullet}(K^{S},\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V))
are strict,
so taking continuous duals is exact.
Since the continuous dual of this complex is precisely
C^{\bullet}(K^{S},\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V)^{\prime})
by a continuous analogue of
Equation

5.1
, taking cohomology gives the second statement.

By
Proposition

5.12
, there is a spectral sequence

E^{2}_{i,j}=\text{Tor}^{\mathcal{H}_{S,E}^{-}}_{i}(E(1),H_{j}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma})[\mathfrak{m}_{1}^{\infty}])\implies H_{i+j}(K^{S},\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(V)).

Dualising and using the same arguments as before to restrict to generalised eigenspaces, it’s enough show that the dual of
\text{Tor}^{\mathcal{H}_{S,E}^{-}}_{i}(E(1),H_{j}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma})[\mathfrak{m}_{1}^{\infty}])
is precisely
\text{Ext}_{\mathcal{H}_{S,E}^{-}}^{i}(E(1),H^{j}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma})[\mathfrak{m}_{1}^{\infty}])
.
This is straightforward and can be seen by computing the Tor and Ext groups with a finite resolution of
E(1)
and noting that the same argument as in the previous paragraph shows that the dual of
H_{j}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma})[\mathfrak{m}_{1}^{\infty}]
is isomorphic to
H^{j}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma})[\mathfrak{m}_{1}^{\infty}]
(and using again that passing to duals is exact).
Note that the generalised eigenspace given by the
\mathfrak{m}_{1}^{\infty}
-torsion of
H_{j}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma})
is a direct summand of the latter, and hence its dual is the
\mathfrak{m}_{1}^{\infty}
-torsion of the dual space
H^{j}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma})
.
∎

Corollary 5.15
.

(i)The vector space
H^{*}(K^{S},\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V)^{\prime})
is finite-dimensional.

(ii)

H^{*}(K^{S},\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V)^{\prime})
vanishes unless
1
is an eigenvalue of
U_{\mu}
in
H^{*}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma})
for all
\mu\in\mathpzc{M}^{-}
.

(iii)

Assume that for some choice of norm on the Banach space
U\otimes_{E}\sigma
, some element
\mu
of
\mathpzc{M}^{+}
acts as an operator of norm
<1
. Then
H^{*}(K^{S},\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V)^{\prime})=0
.

Recall that in
Section

4.2
we defined subspaces
\mathcal{A}^{(s,s^{\prime})-{\Sigma-\text{an}}}_{\sigma}
of
\Ind_{J\cap P}^{J}(\sigma)^{\Sigma-\text{la}}
for
s\geq s_{\sigma}
and
s^{\prime}\geq s^{\prime}_{\sigma}
. Set
\mathcal{A}^{(s,s^{\prime})-{\Sigma-\text{an}}}_{\sigma,U}=U\otimes_{R}\mathcal{A}^{(s,s^{\prime})-{\Sigma-\text{an}}}_{\sigma}
. Recall also from
Corollary

2.32
that there is an
E
-algebra isomorphism
\mathcal{H}_{S,E}^{-}\simeq\mathcal{H}(J_{0,s}\mathpzc{M}^{-}J_{0,s},J_{0,s})_{E}
for any
s\geq 0
.

Proposition 5.16
.

There is a natural
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K^{S})\otimes\mathcal{H}_{S,E}^{-}
-equivariant isomorphism

H^{*}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma})[\mathfrak{m}_{1}^{\infty}]\simeq H^{*}(K^{S}J_{0,s},\mathcal{D}_{\sigma_{\Sigma},U}^{(s,0)-{\Sigma-\text{an}}}\otimes_{E}(\sigma^{\Sigma})^{\prime})[\mathfrak{m}_{1}^{\infty}].

Moreover,

H^{*}(K^{S}J_{0,s},\mathcal{D}_{\sigma_{\Sigma},U}^{(s,0)-{\Sigma-\text{an}}}\otimes_{E}(\sigma^{\Sigma})^{\prime})\simeq\left(H^{*}(K^{S}J_{1,s},\mathcal{D}_{\sigma_{\Sigma},U}^{(s,0)-{\Sigma-\text{an}}})\otimes_{E}(\sigma^{\Sigma})^{\prime}\right)^{J_{0,s}/J_{1,s}}.

Proof.
Fix
a\in A_{\Sigma}^{--}
; all slope decompositions will be with respect to
U_{a}
.
We may assume that
\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma}=\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,U}^{s-{\Sigma-\text{an}}}
.
The argument in the proof of
[
Urb11
, Lemma 4.3.6]
shows that

H^{*}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,U}^{s-{\Sigma-\text{an}}})_{\leq 0}\simeq H^{*}(K^{S}J_{0,s},\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,U}^{s-{\Sigma-\text{an}}})_{\leq 0}.

This argument uses the fact that, if
\mu\in\mathpzc{M}^{-}
satisfies
\mu^{-1}N_{s}\mu\subseteq N_{\widetilde{s}}
for some
\widetilde{s}<s
(for example, if
\mu=a
), then by
Proposition

2.21
one has

J_{0,\widetilde{s}}\cap\mu^{-1}J_{0,s}\mu=\overline{N}_{0}M_{0}\mu^{-1}N_{s}\mu=J_{0,s}\cap\mu^{-1}J_{0,s}\mu.

Lemma

4.20
asserts that if
s
is large enough so that
M_{s}
acts trivially on
\sigma^{\Sigma}
then there is an isomorphism of
J_{0,s}\mathpzc{M}^{-}J_{0,s}
-modules

\mathcal{A}_{\sigma,U}^{s-{\Sigma-\text{an}}}\simeq\mathcal{A}_{\sigma_{\Sigma},U}^{s-{\Sigma-\text{an}}}\otimes_{E}\sigma^{\Sigma}.

By
[
Sch02
, Proposition 20.13]
, this implies that
\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,U}^{s-{\Sigma-\text{an}}}\simeq\mathcal{D}_{\sigma_{\Sigma},U}^{s-{\Sigma-\text{an}}}\otimes_{E}(\sigma^{\Sigma})^{\prime}
.
Using
[
Urb11
, Lemma 2.3.13]
we then see that

H^{*}(K^{S}J_{0,s},\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,U}^{s-{\Sigma-\text{an}}})_{\leq 0}\simeq H^{*}(K^{S}J_{0,s},\mathcal{D}_{\sigma_{\Sigma},U}^{(s,s^{\prime})-{\Sigma-\text{an}}}\otimes_{E}(\sigma^{\Sigma})^{\prime})_{\leq 0}

for any
s^{\prime}\leq s
.
In particular, both spaces are isomorphic to

H^{j}(K^{S}J_{0,s},\mathcal{D}_{\sigma_{\Sigma},U}^{(s,0)-{\Sigma-\text{an}}}\otimes_{E}(\sigma^{\Sigma})^{\prime})_{\leq 0},

so taking generalised eigenspaces proves the first isomorphism.
Recall that
J_{0,s}
acts on
\sigma^{\Sigma}
via the surjective homomorphism
J_{0,s}\longrightarrow M_{0}/M_{s}
described above
Corollary

2.23
, whose kernel is
J_{1,s}
. In particular, the restriction of
\sigma^{\Sigma}
(and its dual) to
J_{1,s}
is trivial, so

\displaystyle H^{*}(K^{S}J_{0,s},\mathcal{D}_{\sigma_{\Sigma},U}^{(s,0)-{\Sigma-\text{an}}}\otimes_{E}(\sigma^{\Sigma})^{\prime})

\displaystyle\simeq H^{*}(K^{S}J_{1,s},\mathcal{D}_{\sigma_{\Sigma},U}^{(s,0)-{\Sigma-\text{an}}}\otimes_{E}(\sigma^{\Sigma})^{\prime})^{J_{0,s}/J_{1,s}}

\displaystyle\simeq\left(H^{*}(K^{S}J_{1,s},\mathcal{D}_{\sigma_{\Sigma},U}^{(s,0)-{\Sigma-\text{an}}})\otimes_{E}(\sigma^{\Sigma})^{\prime}\right)^{J_{0,s}/J_{1,s}}.\qed

5.7.
Relation to automorphic representations

We continue to assume that
R=E
is a finite field extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
in
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.
We assume moreover that it is sufficiently large, in the sense that it contains all the embeddings of the splitting fields
L^{\prime}_{v}
of
Section

5.3
.
Recall that we have fixed an isomorphism
\iota\colon\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\mathbb{C}
.
We will use
\iota
to view
\mathbb{C}
as an extension of
E
.
Assume also that
\mathbb{C}\otimes_{E,\iota}\cInd_{\mathpzc{M}}^{M}(\sigma)
is a supercuspidal representation of
M
and that
U
is an irreducible algebraic representation of
G_{\mathcal{S}_{p}}
.
More precisely, assume we are in the following situation.
Recall that in
Section

5.3
we defined
\mathbf{T}_{\mathcal{S}_{p},E}\subseteq\mathbf{B}_{\mathcal{S}_{p},E}\subseteq\mathbf{G}_{\mathcal{S}_{p},E}
.
Choose a dominant weight
\lambda\in X^{*}(\mathbf{T}_{\mathcal{S}_{p},E})
.
This is equivalent to choosing for each
v
and embedding
\tau\colon F_{v}\xhookrightarrow{}E
a dominant weight
\lambda_{v,\tau}\in X^{*}(E\times_{F_{v},\tau}\mathbf{T}_{v})
.
Let
V_{\mathbf{G}_{\mathcal{S}_{p},E}}(\lambda)
be the irreducible representation of
\mathbf{G}_{\mathcal{S}_{p},E}
over
E
of highest weight
\lambda
and
V_{G_{\mathcal{S}_{p}}}(\lambda)
its restriction to
G_{\mathcal{S}_{p}}\subseteq\mathbf{G}_{\mathcal{S}_{p},E}(E)
.
We assume that
U=V_{G_{\mathcal{S}_{p}}}(\lambda)^{\prime}
.
Finally, we will assume also that
\Sigma=\emptyset
and
\sigma_{\Sigma}
is isomorphic to
E
.

Given a vector space
\mathcal{M}
over
E
(or
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
), we will write
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{C}}=\mathbb{C}\otimes_{E,\iota}\mathcal{M}
for its extension of scalars to
\mathbb{C}
via
\iota
.
The representation
\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V)_{\mathbb{C}}
is thus isomorphic to the locally algebraic representation
V_{G_{\mathcal{S}_{p}}}(\lambda)^{\prime}_{\mathbb{C}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\Ind_{P}^{G}(V_{\mathbb{C}})^{\text{sm}}
of
G_{\mathcal{S}_{p}}\times G
over
\mathbb{C}
, and
\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V)^{\prime}_{\mathbb{C}}
is isomorphic to its abstract
\mathbb{C}
-dual (since, before base changing to
\mathbb{C}
,
\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V)
was equipped with its finest locally convex topology).
The weight
\lambda
gives rise to a weight
\lambda_{\mathbb{C}}
of
\mathbb{C}\times_{E,\iota}\mathbf{T}_{\mathcal{S}_{p},E}
, and the irreducible representation of
\mathbb{C}\times_{E,\iota}\mathbf{G}_{\mathcal{S}_{p},E}\simeq\mathbb{C}\times_{\mathbb{Q}}\Res_{F/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbf{G}
of highest weight
\lambda_{\mathbb{C}}
is isomorphic to the base change of
V_{\mathbf{G}_{\mathcal{S}_{p},E}}(\lambda)
.
We will use this to view the latter as a representation of
G_{\infty}
.

Recall (cf.
[
BW00
]
,
[
Fra98
]
,
[
LS04
]
) that for any open compact subgroup
K_{S}\subseteq G_{S}
, the arithmetic cohomology
H^{*}(K^{S}K_{S},V_{G_{\mathcal{S}_{p}}}(\lambda))_{\mathbb{C}}\simeq H^{*}(K^{S}K_{S},V_{G_{\mathcal{S}_{p}}}(\lambda)_{\mathbb{C}})
admits a canonical
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K^{S})_{\mathbb{C}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\mathcal{H}(G_{S},K_{S})_{\mathbb{C}}
-stable decomposition

(5.17)

\displaystyle H^{*}(K^{S}K_{S},V_{G_{\mathcal{S}_{p}}}(\lambda))_{\mathbb{C}}

\displaystyle\simeq H^{*}_{\text{cusp}}(K^{S}K_{S},V_{G_{\mathcal{S}_{p}}}(\lambda))_{\mathbb{C}}\oplus H^{*}_{\text{Eis}}(K^{S}K_{S},V_{G_{\mathcal{S}_{p}}}(\lambda))_{\mathbb{C}},

into so-called
cuspidal
and
Eisenstein
summands, and moreover cuspidal cohomology admits a decomposition

(5.18)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&H^{*}_{{\text{cusp}}}(K^{S}K_{S},V_{G_{\mathcal{S}_{p}}}(\lambda))_{\mathbb{C}}\\
&\simeq\bigoplus_{\pi}\mathbb{C}^{m(\pi)}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}(\pi^{\infty})^{K^{S}K_{S}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}H^{*}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\infty},K_{\infty};\pi_{\infty}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}V_{\mathbf{G}_{\mathcal{S}_{p},E}}(\lambda)_{\mathbb{C}}),\end{split}

where the sum ranges over all cuspidal automorphic representations
\pi
of
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
,
m(\pi)
denotes the multiplicity of
\pi
as a subrepresentation of
L^{2}_{\text{cusp}}(\mathbf{G}(F)\backslash\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{F}))
,
\pi^{\infty}
(resp.
\pi_{\infty}
) is the finite (resp. infinite) component of
\pi
and
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\infty}
is the Lie
\mathbb{R}
-algebra of the intersection of all the kernels of the
\mathbb{Q}
-rational characters of
\Res_{F/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbf{G}
.
If
\pi
to contributes to
H^{*}(K^{S}K_{S},V_{G_{\mathcal{S}_{p}}}(\lambda))_{\mathbb{C}}
(i.e. if the direct summand corresponding to
\pi
is non-zero) we will say that
\pi
is
cohomological of level
K^{S}K_{S}
and weight
\lambda_{\mathbb{C}}
.
For such
\pi
,
\pi^{\infty}
has non-zero
K^{S}K_{S}
-invariant vectors, and
\pi_{\infty}
has the same central character
\omega_{\lambda}
and infinitesimal character as
V_{\mathbf{G}_{\mathcal{S}_{p},E}}(\lambda)^{\prime}_{\mathbb{C}}
.

Proposition

5.16
and the paragraph before it imply that for sufficiently large
s
there is an isomorphism

H^{*}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma})[\mathfrak{m}_{1}^{\infty}]\simeq\left(\left(H^{*}(K^{S}J_{1,s},V_{G_{\mathcal{S}_{p}}}(\lambda))\otimes_{E}\sigma^{\prime}\right)^{J_{0,s}/J_{1,s}}\right)[\mathfrak{m}_{1}^{\infty}].

The decomposition of
H^{*}(K^{S}J_{1,s},V_{G_{\mathcal{S}_{p}}}(\lambda))_{\mathbb{C}}
into cuspidal and Eisenstein summands is compatible with the action of
J_{0,s}/J_{1,s}
.
Applying
\iota
, we may thus define a decomposition

H^{*}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma})_{\mathbb{C}}[\mathfrak{m}_{1,\mathbb{C}}^{\infty}]\simeq H^{*}_{\text{cusp}}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma})_{\mathbb{C}}[\mathfrak{m}_{1,\mathbb{C}}^{\infty}]\oplus H^{*}_{\text{Eis}}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma})_{\mathbb{C}}[\mathfrak{m}_{1,\mathbb{C}}^{\infty}].

and similarly we may write
H^{*}_{\text{cusp}}(K^{S}J,\mathcal{D}_{\sigma,U}^{\Sigma})_{\mathbb{C}}[\mathfrak{m}_{1,\mathbb{C}}^{\infty}]
as a direct sum indexed by cuspidal automorphic representations.

Proposition 5.19
.

When
K_{S}=J_{1,s}
, the spectral sequence in
Proposition

5.14
splits as a direct sum of two analogous spectral sequences involving the cuspidal and Eisenstein parts of arithmetic cohomology.
The cuspidal direct summand spectral sequence degenerates at the
E_{2}
page.

Proof.
In order to prove that the spectral sequence splits as such a direct sum, by its construction it is enough to observe that the decomposition
Equation

5.17
is obtained by taking cohomology of an analogous decomposition in the derived category of
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K^{S})_{\mathbb{C}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\mathcal{H}(G_{S},K_{S})_{\mathbb{C}}
-modules,

\displaystyle C^{\bullet}(K^{S}K_{S},V_{G_{\mathcal{S}_{p}}}(\lambda))_{\mathbb{C}}

\displaystyle\simeq C^{\bullet}_{\text{cusp}}(K^{S}K_{S},V_{G_{\mathcal{S}_{p}}}(\lambda))_{\mathbb{C}}\oplus C^{\bullet}_{\text{Eis}}(K^{S}K_{S},V_{G_{\mathcal{S}_{p}}}(\lambda))_{\mathbb{C}}.

This follows from the work of Franke
[
Fra98
]
, more concretely from
[
Fra98
, Theorem in §2.2 and Theorem 4]
and their proofs.

Let us prove that the cuspidal part degenerates. The decomposition
Equation

5.18
is also obtained from taking cohomology of an isomorphism in the derived category of
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K^{S})_{\mathbb{C}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\mathcal{H}(G_{S},K_{S})_{\mathbb{C}}
-modules, namely

\displaystyle C^{\bullet}_{{\text{cusp}}}(K^{S}K_{S},V_{G_{\mathcal{S}_{p}}}(\lambda))_{\mathbb{C}}

\displaystyle\simeq\text{Hom}_{(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\infty},K_{\infty})}(\bigwedge^{\bullet}\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\infty}/\mathfrak{k}\right),L^{2}_{\text{cusp}}(\mathbf{G}(F)\backslash\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{F}),\omega_{\lambda})^{K^{S}K_{S}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}V_{\mathbf{G}_{\mathcal{S}_{p},E}}(\lambda)_{\mathbb{C}}),

\displaystyle\simeq\bigoplus_{\pi}\mathbb{C}^{m(\pi)}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}(\pi^{\infty})^{K^{S}K_{S}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\text{Hom}_{(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\infty},K_{\infty})}(\bigwedge^{\bullet}\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\infty}/\mathfrak{k}\right),\pi_{\infty}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}V_{\mathbf{G}_{\mathcal{S}_{p},E}}(\lambda)_{\mathbb{C}}).

The latter complex is, as a complex in the derived category of
\mathcal{H}(G_{S},K_{S})_{\mathbb{C}}
-modules, isomorphic to the direct sum of its shifted cohomologies.
In particular, this induces a decomposition of the cuspidal part of the spectral sequence from
Proposition

5.14
as a direct sum of spectral sequences that collapse at the
E_{2}
page.
∎

This proposition has a few important consequences for
S
-arithmetic cohomology. For example, we can use it to define a decomposition

H^{*}(K^{S},\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V)^{\prime})_{\mathbb{C}}\simeq H^{*}_{\text{cusp}}(K^{S},\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V)^{\prime})_{\mathbb{C}}\oplus H^{*}_{\text{Eis}}(K^{S},\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V)^{\prime})_{\mathbb{C}},

each summand being the abutment of the corresponding spectral sequence.
Moreover, the proof of the proposition allows us to give a very explicit description of the cuspidal part.
Given a cuspidal automorphic representation
\pi
, we will write
\pi=\bigotimes^{\prime}_{v}\pi_{v}
,
\pi_{S}=\bigotimes_{v\in S}\pi_{v}
and
\pi^{S\infty}=\bigotimes^{\prime}_{v\not\in S,v\nmid\infty}\pi_{v}
.
There is thus a spectral sequence converging to
H^{*}_{\text{cusp}}(K^{S},\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V)^{\prime})_{\mathbb{C}}
with terms

\displaystyle E_{2}^{i,j}=\bigoplus_{\pi}

\displaystyle\mathbb{C}^{m(\pi)}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}(\pi^{S\infty})^{K^{S}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}H^{j}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\infty},K_{\infty};\pi_{\infty}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}V_{G_{\mathcal{S}_{p}}}(\lambda)_{\mathbb{C}})

\displaystyle\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\text{Ext}_{\mathcal{H}_{S,\mathbb{C}}^{-}}^{i}\left({\mathbb{C}}(1),(\pi_{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}^{\prime})^{J_{0,s}/J_{1,s}}\right),

where the sum ranges over all cuspidal
\pi
appearing in
Equation

5.18
for
K_{S}=J_{1,s}
, which not only degenerates at the
E_{2}
page, but is in fact a direct sum of spectral sequences that
collapse
at the
E^{2}
page.

In particular, we can decompose
H^{*}_{\text{cusp}}(K^{S},\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V)^{\prime})_{\mathbb{C}}
as a direct sum indexed by cuspidal automorphic representations
\pi
.
If
\pi
contributes to this direct sum, then the (honest) eigenspace for 1 and the action of
\mathcal{H}_{S,\mathbb{C}}
in
(\pi_{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}^{\prime})^{J_{0,s}/J_{1,s}}
is non-zero.
This space is isomorphic to
\text{Hom}_{J_{0,s}}(\sigma_{\mathbb{C}},\pi_{S})=\text{Hom}_{J_{0,s}}(\sigma_{\mathbb{C}},\pi_{S}^{J_{1,s}})
.
Because the action of
J_{1,s}
on
\sigma
is trivial and a set of representatives for
J_{1,s}\mu J_{1,s}/J_{1,s}
will also be a set of representatives for
J_{0,s}\mu J_{0,s}/J_{0,s}
for any
\mu\in\mathpzc{M}^{-}
, any eigenfunction for the operators
U_{\mu}
with
\mu\in\mathpzc{M}^{-}
in
\text{Hom}_{J_{0,s}}(\sigma_{\mathbb{C}},\pi_{S})
must take values in
[J_{1,s}aJ_{1,s}]\pi_{S}^{J_{1,s}}
for all
a\in A^{-}
(since the image of
U_{a}f
is contained in this subspace).
Recall from
[
Cas
, Proposition 4.1.4]
that there is a natural isomorphism of vector spaces over
\mathbb{C}

[J_{1,s}aJ_{1,s}]\pi_{S}^{J_{1,s}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}(\pi_{S})_{\overline{N}}^{M_{s}}

where
(\pi_{S})_{\overline{N}}
denotes the (non-normalised) Jacquet module of
\pi_{S}
with respect to
\overline{N}
,
and that for
m\in M^{-}
, the Hecke operator
[J_{1,s}mJ_{1,s}]
on the left-hand side is identified with the action of
m
twisted by the modulus character
\delta_{P}
of
P
.
It follows that non-zero eigenfunctions in
\text{Hom}_{J_{0,s}}(\sigma_{\mathbb{C}},\pi_{S})
of eigenvalue 1 for the operators
U_{\mu}
are in bijection with non-zero element of
\text{Hom}_{\mathpzc{M}^{-}}(\sigma_{\mathbb{C}},(\pi_{S})_{\overline{N}}\otimes\delta_{P})
.
Consequently,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\neq\text{Hom}_{\mathpzc{M}^{-}}(\sigma_{\mathbb{C}},(\pi_{S})_{\overline{N}}\otimes\delta_{P})

\displaystyle=\text{Hom}_{\mathpzc{M}}(\sigma_{\mathbb{C}},(\pi_{S})_{\overline{N}}\otimes\delta_{P})

\displaystyle=\text{Hom}_{M}(V_{\mathbb{C}}\otimes\delta_{P}^{-1},(\pi_{S})_{\overline{N}})

\displaystyle=\text{Hom}_{M}((\widetilde{\pi_{S}})_{N},\widetilde{V}_{\mathbb{C}}\otimes\delta_{P}),

where we use
\widetilde{({-})}
to denote smooth contragredients.
For the last equality we have used the fact that
\widetilde{(\pi_{S})_{\overline{N}}}\simeq(\widetilde{\pi_{S}})_{N}
by
[
Cas
, Corollary 4.2.5]
.
Putting everything together, we have proved the following theorem, which is a more precise version of
Theorem

1.1
.

Theorem 5.20
.

There is a
\mathbb{T}^{S}(K^{S})_{\mathbb{C}}
-equivariant decomposition

H^{*}_{\text{cusp}}(K^{S},V_{G_{\mathcal{S}_{p}}}(\lambda)_{\mathbb{C}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}(\Ind_{P}^{G}(V_{\mathbb{C}})^{\text{sm}})^{\prime})_{\mathbb{C}}\simeq\bigoplus_{\pi}\mathbb{C}^{m(\pi)}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}(\pi^{S\infty})^{K^{S}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}H^{*}_{\pi}

where
\pi
ranges over (cohomological) cuspidal automorphic representations
\pi
of
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{F})
of level
K^{S}
away from
S
, weight
\lambda_{\mathbb{C}}
, and such that
\widetilde{V}_{\mathbb{C}}\otimes\delta_{P}
is a quotient of the Jacquet module with respect to
P
of
\widetilde{\pi_{S}}
(or equivalently,
V_{\mathbb{C}}\otimes\delta_{\overline{P}}
is a subrepresentation of the Jacquet module of
\pi_{S}
with respect to
\overline{P}
), where for all
n
,

H^{n}_{\pi}\simeq\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}H^{n-i}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\infty},K_{\infty};\pi_{\infty}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}V_{G_{\mathcal{S}_{p}}}(\lambda)_{\mathbb{C}})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\text{Ext}_{\mathcal{H}_{S,\mathbb{C}}^{-}}^{i}\left({\mathbb{C}}(1),(\pi_{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\sigma_{\mathbb{C}}^{\prime})^{J_{0,s}}\right).

5.8.
Orlik–Strauch representations and classicality

We continue to assume that
E
is as in last section, but impose no algebraicity or supercuspidality conditions on
U,\sigma
.
We will assume for simplicity that
\sigma
is smooth as this case is sufficient for our purposes.
Let
W_{S_{p},E}
denote the Weyl group of
\mathbf{G}_{S_{p},E}
with respect to
\mathbf{T}_{S_{p},E}
, and define
\Delta_{S_{p},E}
and
\Delta_{S_{p},M,E}
similarly. We make analogous definitions for subsets of
S_{p}
.
Recall also that the
dot-action
of
W_{S_{p},E}
on the set of weights
X^{*}(\mathbf{T}_{S_{p},E})
is defined by
w\cdot\lambda=w(\lambda+\rho)-\rho
where
\rho
is the half-sum of positive roots of
\mathbf{G}_{S_{p},E}
. In particular, this action depends on our choice of Borel subgroup
\mathbf{B}_{S_{p},E}
.

Let
\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{m}
and
\mathfrak{p}
denote the Lie
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-algebras of
G_{S_{p}},M_{S_{p}}
and
P_{S_{p}}
respectively and
U(\mathfrak{g}),U(\mathfrak{m})
and
U(\mathfrak{p})
their universal enveloping algebra. Let
\mathfrak{g}_{E}=E\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathfrak{g}
be the Lie
E
-algebra of
\mathbf{G}_{S_{p},E}
, and similarly for
\mathfrak{m}_{E},\mathfrak{p}_{E},U(\mathfrak{g}_{E}),U(\mathfrak{p}_{E})
and
U(\mathfrak{m}_{E})
.
Consider the categories
\mathcal{O}^{\mathfrak{p}_{E}}
and
\mathcal{O}^{\mathfrak{p}_{E}}_{\text{alg}}
of
U(\mathfrak{g}_{E})
-modules defined in
[
OS14
]
, and write
\text{Rep}^{\text{sm,adm}}(M_{S_{p}})
(resp.
\text{Rep}^{\text{la}}(G_{S_{p}})
) for the category of smooth admissible representations of
M_{S_{p}}
(resp. locally
S_{p}
-analytic representations of
G
) over
E
. Following the strategy of Orlik and Strauch
[
OS14
]
we may define a functor

\mathcal{F}_{P_{S_{p}}}^{G_{S_{p}}}\colon(\mathcal{O}^{\mathfrak{p}_{E}}_{\text{alg}})^{\text{op}}\times\text{Rep}^{\text{sm,adm}}(M_{S_{p}})\longrightarrow\text{Rep}^{\text{la}}(G_{S_{p}})

which is exact in both arguments.
We refer to the appendix for its construction and the proof of these properties when the group is non-split.

Example 5.21
.

Let
\lambda\in X^{*}(\mathbf{T}_{S_{p},E})
be a dominant weight for
\mathbf{M}_{S_{p},E}
(with respect to
\mathbf{M}_{S_{p},E}\cap\mathbf{B}_{S_{p},E}
).
Let
V_{M_{S_{p}}}(\lambda)
be the restriction to
M_{S_{p}}=\mathbf{M}_{S_{p}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\subseteq\mathbf{M}_{S_{p},E}(E)
of the irreducible algebraic representation of
\mathbf{M}_{S_{p},E}
of highest weight
\lambda
.
We may view
V_{M_{S_{p}}}(\lambda)
as a representation of
\mathfrak{m}_{E}
, and of
\mathfrak{p}_{E}
by inflation.
Let
\mathcal{M}_{P_{S_{p}}}^{G_{S_{p}}}(\lambda):=U(\mathfrak{g}_{E})\otimes_{U(\mathfrak{p}_{E})}V_{M_{S_{p}}}(\lambda)\in\mathcal{O}^{\mathfrak{p}_{E}}_{\text{alg}}
be the parabolic Verma module of highest weight
\lambda
.
Then,

\mathcal{F}_{P_{S_{p}}}^{G_{S_{p}}}(\mathcal{M}_{P_{S_{p}}}^{G_{S_{p}}}(\lambda),V_{S_{p}})\simeq\Ind_{P_{S_{p}}}^{G_{S_{p}}}(V_{M_{S_{p}}}(\lambda)^{\prime}\otimes_{E}V_{S_{p}})^{\text{la}},

(recall that
V_{S_{p}}
is defined in
Section

5.3
) and if
\lambda
is dominant for
\mathbf{G}_{S_{p},E}
, then

\mathcal{F}_{P_{S_{p}}}^{G_{S_{p}}}(V_{G_{S_{p}}}(\lambda),V_{S_{p}})\simeq V_{G_{S_{p}}}(\lambda)^{\prime}\otimes_{E}\Ind_{P_{S_{p}}}^{G_{S_{p}}}(V_{S_{p}})^{\text{sm}}.

The natural projection
\mathcal{M}_{P_{S_{p}}}^{G_{S_{p}}}(\lambda)\twoheadrightarrow V_{G_{S_{p}}}(\lambda)
induces the natural inclusion

V_{G_{S_{p}}}(\lambda)^{\prime}\otimes_{E}\Ind_{P_{S_{p}}}^{G_{S_{p}}}(V_{S_{p}})^{\text{sm}}\xhookrightarrow{}\Ind_{P_{S_{p}}}^{G_{S_{p}}}(V_{M_{S_{p}}}(\lambda)^{\prime}\otimes_{E}V_{S_{p}})^{\text{la}}

of locally algebraic induction into locally analytic induction. More generally, for any
\Sigma\subseteq S_{p}
we may write
\lambda=(\lambda_{\Sigma},\lambda^{\Sigma})\in X^{*}(\mathbf{T}_{\Sigma,E})\times X^{*}(\mathbf{T}_{S_{p}\smallsetminus\Sigma,E})

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{P_{S_{p}}}^{G_{S_{p}}}(\mathcal{M}_{P_{\Sigma}\times G_{S_{p}\smallsetminus\Sigma}}^{G_{S_{p}}}(\lambda),V_{S_{p}})

\displaystyle\simeq V_{G_{S_{p}\smallsetminus\Sigma}}(\lambda^{\Sigma})^{\prime}\otimes_{E}\Ind_{P_{S_{p}}}^{G_{S_{p}}}(V_{M_{\Sigma}}(\lambda_{\Sigma})^{\prime}\otimes_{E}V_{S_{p}})^{\Sigma-\text{la}}

\displaystyle=\SigmaInd_{P_{S_{p}}}^{G_{S_{p}}}(V_{G_{S_{p}\smallsetminus\Sigma}}(\lambda^{\Sigma})^{\prime},V_{M_{\Sigma}}(\lambda_{\Sigma})^{\prime}\otimes_{E}V_{S_{p}}).

Let us remark that, for any locally convex space
W
of compact type, the bifunctor
\mathcal{F}_{P_{S_{p}}}^{G_{S_{p}}}({-},{-})\cotimes_{E}W
is also exact in both arguments and in
W
(by
[
Eme17
, Proposition 1.1.26]
, reflexivity, and the exactness of passing to duals).
We will abuse notation and write

\mathcal{F}_{P}^{G}(X,V):=\mathcal{F}_{P_{S_{p}}}^{G_{S_{p}}}(X,V_{S_{p}})\cotimes_{E}\Ind_{P_{S\smallsetminus S_{p}}}^{G_{S\smallsetminus S_{p}}}(V^{S_{p}})^{\text{sm}}.

This is still exact in both arguments.

Proposition 5.22
.

Let
X\in\mathcal{O}^{\mathfrak{p}_{E}}_{\text{alg}}
. Then, the
S
-arithmetic homology space

H_{*}(K^{S},U\otimes_{E}\mathcal{F}_{P}^{G}(X,V))

is finite-dimensional, and similarly for the cohomology of the dual representation.

Proof.
The Verma modules
\mathcal{M}_{P_{S_{p}}}^{G_{S_{p}}}(\lambda)
form a basis of the Grothendieck group of the (Artinian) category
\mathcal{O}^{\mathfrak{p}_{E}}_{\text{alg}}
as
\lambda
runs through the set of dominant weights for
\mathbf{M}_{S_{p},E}
. Thus, the image of the representation
X
in the Grothendieck group of
\mathcal{O}^{\mathfrak{p}_{E}}_{\text{alg}}
may be written as a finite sum

[X]=\sum_{i=1}^{r}[\mathcal{M}_{P_{S_{p}}}^{G_{S_{p}}}(\lambda_{i})]-\sum_{i=r+1}^{s}[\mathcal{M}_{P_{S_{p}}}^{G_{S_{p}}}(\lambda_{i})].

Therefore, the proposition follows by induction on
s
by the exactness of the functor
U\otimes_{E}\mathcal{F}_{P}^{G}({-},V)
and passing to continuous duals,
Corollary

5.13
,
Corollary

5.15
and the long exact sequences in (co)homology associated to a short exact sequence.
∎

Our next goal is to prove
Theorem

1.4
from the introduction.
For this, we want to relate the
S
-arithmetic homology of the various representations
\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V)
as we vary
\Sigma
(and also
U
and
V
accordingly).
Let
\widetilde{\Sigma}\subseteq\Sigma
be a subset.
In our application to
Theorem

1.4
we will only need the case where
\widetilde{\Sigma}
is the empty set, so the reader is welcome to assume this.
Write
W_{\Sigma,M,E}
(resp.
\Delta_{\Sigma,M,E}
) for the Weyl group (resp. root basis) of
\mathbf{M}_{\Sigma,E}
and similarly for other subsets of
S_{p}
and Levi subgroups of parabolics.
When the Levi subgroup is the group
G
itself, we will omit it from the notation.
Define
{}^{M}W_{\Sigma\smallsetminus\widetilde{\Sigma},E}:={}^{M_{\Sigma}}W_{\Sigma,M_{\widetilde{\Sigma}}\times G_{\Sigma\smallsetminus\widetilde{\Sigma}},E}
as in
Section

2.8
, i.e. as the set of minimal length representatives of cosets in
W_{\Sigma,M_{\Sigma},E}\backslash W_{\Sigma,M_{\widetilde{\Sigma}}\times G_{\Sigma\smallsetminus\widetilde{\Sigma}},E}
.
Note that this coset set is naturally in bijection with
W_{\Sigma\smallsetminus\widetilde{\Sigma},M,E}\backslash W_{\Sigma\smallsetminus\widetilde{\Sigma},E}
, which will hopefully justify the notation.
Similarly,
\Delta_{\Sigma,M_{\widetilde{\Sigma}}\times G_{\Sigma\smallsetminus\widetilde{\Sigma}},E}\smallsetminus\Delta_{\Sigma,M_{\Sigma},E}\simeq\Delta_{\Sigma\smallsetminus\widetilde{\Sigma},E}\smallsetminus\Delta_{\Sigma\smallsetminus\widetilde{\Sigma},M,E}
.

Definition 5.23
.

Assume that
A_{\Sigma}
acts on
U\otimes_{E}V
via a character
\chi
.
Let
\lambda
be a dominant regular weight of
\mathbf{M}_{\widetilde{\Sigma}}\times\mathbf{G}_{\Sigma\smallsetminus\widetilde{\Sigma}}
.
We say that
A_{\Sigma}
acts numerically non-critically (with respect to
\widetilde{\Sigma}\subseteq\Sigma
and
\mathbf{P}
) on the representation
U\otimes_{E}V_{M_{\Sigma}}(\lambda)^{\prime}\otimes_{E}V
if for any simple reflection
w
corresponding to an element of
\Delta_{\Sigma\smallsetminus\widetilde{\Sigma},E}\smallsetminus\Delta_{\Sigma\smallsetminus\widetilde{\Sigma},M,E}
, there exists
a\in A_{\Sigma}^{-}
such that

(5.24)

\displaystyle v_{p}(\chi(a))<v_{p}((w\cdot\lambda)(a)).

Remark 5.25
.

With all notations as in
Definition

5.23
, the inequality
Equation

5.24
holds for all
w\in{}^{M}W_{\Sigma\smallsetminus\widetilde{\Sigma},E}\smallsetminus\{1\}
(for some
a
depending of
w
) by the proof of
Lemma

2.36
.
Indeed, given
w
, we may choose
\alpha\in\Delta_{\Sigma\smallsetminus\widetilde{\Sigma},E}\smallsetminus\Delta_{\Sigma\smallsetminus\widetilde{\Sigma},M,E}
as in that proof and
a\in A_{\Sigma}^{-}
for which
Equation

5.24
is valid for
s_{\alpha}
.
Then, it is valid also for
w
(as the proof of
Lemma

2.36
shows).

Theorem

1.4
follows from applying the following more general theorem when
\widetilde{\Sigma}
is empty and
U
is a finite-dimensional irreducible algebraic representation of
G_{\mathcal{S}_{p}\smallsetminus\Sigma}
.

Theorem 5.26
.

Assume that
A_{\Sigma}
acts on
U\otimes_{E}V
via a character
\chi
.
Let
\lambda=(\lambda_{\widetilde{\Sigma}},\lambda_{\Sigma\smallsetminus\widetilde{\Sigma}})
be a dominant regular weight of
\mathbf{M}_{\widetilde{\Sigma}}\times\mathbf{G}_{\Sigma\smallsetminus\widetilde{\Sigma}}
such that
A_{\Sigma}
acts numerically non-critically on
U\otimes_{E}V_{M_{\Sigma}}(\lambda)^{\prime}\otimes_{E}V
.
Then the natural homomorphism from

H_{*}(K^{S},\widetilde{\Sigma}\text{-Ind}_{P}^{G}(U\otimes_{E}V_{G_{\Sigma\smallsetminus\widetilde{\Sigma}}}(\lambda_{\Sigma\smallsetminus\widetilde{\Sigma}})^{\prime},V_{M_{\widetilde{\Sigma}}}(\lambda_{\widetilde{\Sigma}})^{\prime}\otimes_{E}V))

to

H_{*}(K^{S},\Sigma\text{-Ind}_{P}^{G}(U,V_{M_{\Sigma}}(\lambda_{\Sigma})^{\prime}\otimes_{E}V))

is an isomorphism, and the same is true for the cohomology of the duals.

Proof.
The statement for cohomology is analogous to the one for homology, so let us only prove the latter.
Note that the condition that
A_{\Sigma}
acts on
U\otimes_{E}V
via
\chi
is equivalent to the condition that it acts on
U\otimes_{E}\sigma
via
\chi
.
By
Example

5.21
,
it’s enough to show that the natural map

H_{*}(K^{S},U\otimes_{E}\mathcal{F}_{P}^{G}(\mathcal{M}_{P_{\widetilde{\Sigma}}\times G_{S_{p}\smallsetminus\widetilde{\Sigma}}}^{G_{S_{p}}}(\lambda),V))\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}H_{*}(K^{S},U\otimes_{E}\mathcal{F}_{P}^{G}(\mathcal{M}_{P_{\Sigma}\times G_{S_{p}\smallsetminus\Sigma}}^{G_{S_{p}}}(\lambda),V))

is an isomorphism.

Consider the parabolic BGG resolution of the representation
V_{G_{\Sigma\smallsetminus\widetilde{\Sigma}}}(\lambda_{\Sigma\smallsetminus\widetilde{\Sigma}})
of the Lie algebra of
\mathbf{G}_{\Sigma\smallsetminus\widetilde{\Sigma},E}
,

\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in{}^{M}W_{\Sigma\smallsetminus\widetilde{\Sigma},E}\\
\ell(w)=\bullet\end{subarray}}\mathcal{M}_{P_{\Sigma\smallsetminus\widetilde{\Sigma}}}^{G_{\Sigma\smallsetminus\widetilde{\Sigma}}}(w\cdot\lambda_{\Sigma\smallsetminus\widetilde{\Sigma}})\longrightarrow V_{M_{\Sigma\smallsetminus\widetilde{\Sigma}}}(\lambda_{\Sigma\smallsetminus\widetilde{\Sigma}})\longrightarrow 0.

Tensoring with
\mathcal{M}_{P_{\widetilde{\Sigma}}\times G_{S_{p}\smallsetminus\Sigma}}^{G_{S_{p}\smallsetminus(\Sigma\smallsetminus\widetilde{\Sigma})}}(\lambda_{\widetilde{\Sigma}})
(where we extend
\lambda_{\widetilde{\Sigma}}
to a weight of
\mathbf{M}_{\widetilde{\Sigma}}\times\mathbf{G}_{S_{p}\smallsetminus\Sigma}
trivially), we obtain an exact sequence in
\mathcal{O}^{\mathfrak{p}_{E}}_{\text{alg}}

(5.27)

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in{}^{M}W_{\Sigma\smallsetminus\widetilde{\Sigma},E}\\
\ell(w)=\bullet\end{subarray}}\mathcal{M}_{P_{\Sigma}\times G_{S_{p}\smallsetminus\Sigma}}^{G_{S_{p}}}(w\cdot\lambda)\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}_{P_{\widetilde{\Sigma}}\times G_{S_{p}\smallsetminus\widetilde{\Sigma}}}^{G_{S_{p}}}(\lambda)\longrightarrow 0.

Applying the exact functor
U\otimes_{E}\mathcal{F}_{P}^{G}({-},V)
and taking
S
-arithmetic homology, we obtain a converging spectral sequence

\displaystyle E^{1}_{i,j}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in{}^{M}W_{\Sigma\smallsetminus\widetilde{\Sigma},E}\\
\ell(w)=j\end{subarray}}H_{i}(K^{S},U\otimes_{E}\mathcal{F}_{P}^{G}(\mathcal{M}_{P_{\Sigma}\times G_{S_{p}\smallsetminus\Sigma}}^{G_{S_{p}}}(w\cdot\lambda),V))

\displaystyle\implies H_{i+j}(K^{S},U\otimes_{E}\mathcal{F}_{P}^{G}(\mathcal{M}_{P_{\widetilde{\Sigma}}\times G_{S_{p}\smallsetminus\widetilde{\Sigma}}}^{G_{S_{p}}}(\lambda),V)).

Hence, it’s enough to show that the representations

U\otimes_{E}\mathcal{F}_{P}^{G}(\mathcal{M}_{P_{\Sigma}\times G_{S_{p}\smallsetminus\Sigma}}^{G_{S_{p}}}(w\cdot\lambda),V)=\SigmaInd_{P}^{G}(U,V_{M_{\Sigma}}(w\cdot\lambda)^{\prime}\otimes_{E}V)

have vanishing
S
-arithmetic homology for
w\in{}^{M}W_{\Sigma\smallsetminus\widetilde{\Sigma},E}
,
w\neq 1
.
Let
w\neq 1
and let
a
be as in
Remark

5.25
.
By
Corollary

5.13
(iii), it’s enough to show that
a
acts on
U\otimes_{E}V_{M_{\Sigma}}(w\cdot\lambda)^{\prime}\otimes_{E}\sigma

as an operator of norm strictly greater than one.
But
a
acts by multiplication by

\chi(a)(w\cdot\lambda)(a)^{-1}
, and the numerical non-criticality condition (taking into account
Remark

5.25
) states precisely that this has norm strictly greater than one.
∎

Let us remark that we could have proven this statement by working exclusively with overconvergent homology (where similar criterions are known to hold in many cases, cf.
[
SW21
, Section 4]
) and using the spectral sequences relating it to
S
-arithmetic homology, but this would require us to work with analogues of the locally analytic BGG resolution (i.e. the exact sequence obtained by applying
U\otimes_{E}\mathcal{F}_{P}^{G}({-},V)
to
Equation

5.27
) for representations of
J
in an
ad hoc
manner.

5.9.
The case of
\text{GL}_{n}

In this section, we will prove
Theorem

1.2
.
We will expand on the results of the previous section and prove that, when
\mathbf{G}_{F_{v}}
is isomorphic to
\text{GL}_{n/F_{v}}
for all
v\in S
, all the representation in the image of the functor
\mathcal{F}_{P}^{G}
where the second argument has finite length have finite-dimensional
S
-arithmetic homology. We will deduce this result from the case of the smooth parabolic induction of a supercuspidal representation by using Bernstein–Zelevinsky theory (especially
[
Zel80
]
).
As in the previous section, given

X\in\mathcal{O}^{\mathfrak{p}_{E}}_{\text{alg}}
,
\pi_{S_{p}}\in\text{Rep}^{\text{sm},\text{adm}}(M_{S_{p}})
and
\pi^{S_{p}}\in\text{Rep}^{\text{sm},\text{adm}}(M^{S_{p}})
, we will abuse notation and write
\mathcal{F}_{P}^{G}(X,\pi_{S_{p}}\otimes\pi^{S_{p}}):=\mathcal{F}_{P_{S_{p}}}^{G_{S_{p}}}(X,\pi_{S_{p}})\widehat{\otimes}\Ind_{P_{S\smallsetminus S_{p}}}^{G_{S\smallsetminus S_{p}}}(\pi^{S_{p}})^{\text{sm}}
.
We will now introduce some notation exclusive to this section.

Let
\ell
be a prime,
L
a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}
.
If
\rho=\rho_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\rho_{r}
is a smooth representation of
\text{GL}_{n_{1}}(L)\times\cdots\times\text{GL}_{n_{r}}(L)
, viewed as the Levi subgroup of block-diagonal matrices in
\text{GL}_{n}
for
n=n_{1}+\cdots+n_{r}
of a standard parabolic
\mathbb{P}
(containing the upper-triangular matrices) in the usual way, we will write
\rho_{1}\times\cdots\times\rho_{r}
to denote
normalised
smooth parabolic induction of
\rho
with respect to
P
, i.e.

i(\rho)=\Ind_{\mathbb{P}(L)}^{\text{GL}_{n}(L)}((\rho_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\rho_{r})\otimes\delta_{\mathbb{P}}^{1/2})^{\text{sm}}

where
\delta_{\mathbb{P}}
is the modulus character of
\mathbb{P}
.
If
\rho
is a supercuspidal representation of
\text{GL}_{n}(L)
, we will write
\rho(s)=\rho|\det|^{s-1}
.
By
[
Zel80
, Proposition 2.10]
, the representation
\rho(1)\times\rho(2)\times\cdots\times\rho(s)
of
\text{GL}_{ns}(L)
has a unique irreducible representation
Z_{s}(\rho)
.

Lemma 5.28
.

There is an exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow Z_{s}(\rho)\longrightarrow Z_{s-1}(\rho)\times\rho(s)\longrightarrow\rho(s)\times Z_{s-1}(\rho)\longrightarrow Z_{s}(\rho)\longrightarrow 0.

Proof.
This follows from the results of
[
Zel80
]
, as we now explain.
Let us recall some terminology and facts from
loc. cit.

A set of the form
\Delta=\{\rho(1),...,\rho(s)\}
is called a
segment
.
To such a segment
\Delta
we can associate a graph
\Gamma
, which is a line segment with
s
vertices corresponding to
\rho(1),...,\rho(s)
(in this order).
An orientation of
\Gamma
is a choice of direction for each edge of
\Gamma
.
Any bijection
\lambda\colon\{1,...,s\}\longrightarrow\Delta
(which may be seen as an ordering of
\Delta
) determines an ordering of
\Gamma
: the edge between
\rho(i)
and
\rho(i+1)
goes from
\rho(i)
to
\rho(i+1)
if
\lambda^{-1}(\rho(i))<\lambda^{-1}(\rho(i+1))
.
Every orientation arises from such a
\lambda
.
Let

\pi(\lambda)=\lambda(1)\times\cdots\times\lambda(s)
.
The orientation of
\Gamma
determined by
\lambda
determines the isomorphism class of
\pi(\lambda)
and conversely.
Then,
[
Zel80
, Theorem 2.2 and 2.8]
state that there is a bijection between the set of irreducible constituents of
\pi(\lambda)
(which does not depend on
\lambda
) and the set orientations of
\Gamma
, which sends the orientation corresponding to
\lambda
to the unique irreducible subrepresentation of
\pi(\lambda)
.
In fact, by
[
Zel80
, Theorem 2.8]
this extends to an isomorphism between the lattice of submodules of
\pi(\lambda)
and the sublattice of the set of orientations of
\Gamma
generated by the subsets
\mathcal{O}(\lambda,k)
of orientations such that the edge between
\rho(k)
and
\rho(k+1)
is oriented in the same way as in the orientation corresponding to
\lambda
, for
k=1,...,s-1
.
More explicitly, for
k=1,...,s-1
, there exists some
\lambda^{\prime}
giving the same orientation as
\lambda
and such that for some
j
, either
\lambda^{\prime}(j)=\rho(k)
and
\lambda^{\prime}(j+1)=\rho(k+1)
, or
\lambda^{\prime}(j)=\rho(k+1)
and
\lambda^{\prime}(j+1)=\rho(k)
(depending on the orientation of the edge between these two representations), and
\mathcal{O}(\lambda,k)
corresponds to

\pi(\lambda,k)=\lambda^{\prime}(1)\times\cdots\times\lambda^{\prime}(j-1)\times Z_{2}(\rho)|\det|^{k-1}\times\lambda^{\prime}(j+2)\times\cdots\times\lambda^{\prime}(s)\subseteq\pi(\lambda^{\prime})\simeq\pi(\lambda).

In particular, all proper subrepresentations of
\pi(\lambda)
are iterated sums and intersections of the subrepresentations
\pi(\lambda,k)
.
As an example, consider the bijection
\lambda_{0}\colon\{1,...,s\}\longrightarrow\Delta
given by
\lambda_{0}(i):=\rho(i)
. The corresponding orientation is

\bullet\longrightarrow\bullet\cdots\bullet\longrightarrow\bullet.

Then,
Z_{s}(\rho)=\bigcap_{k=1}^{s-1}\pi(\lambda_{0},k)
and
Z_{s-1}(\rho)\times\rho(s)=\bigcap_{k=1}^{s-2}\pi(\lambda_{0},k)
– these correspond to the sets of orientations

\displaystyle\{\bullet\longrightarrow\bullet\cdots\bullet\longrightarrow\bullet\},

\displaystyle\{\bullet\longrightarrow\bullet\cdots\bullet\longrightarrow\bullet,~~~\bullet\longrightarrow\cdots\bullet\longrightarrow\bullet\longleftarrow\bullet\}

respectively.
In particular, the quotient
Q_{s}(\rho)
of
Z_{s-1}(\rho)\times\rho(s)
by
Z_{s}(\rho)
is irreducible. In fact,
Q_{s}(\rho)
is isomorphic to the unique irreducible submodule of
\pi(\mu)
, where
\mu
is defined by
\mu(i)=\rho(i-1)
if
i\geq 2
and
\mu(1)=\rho(s)
, and has corresponding orientation

\bullet\longrightarrow\bullet\cdots\bullet\longrightarrow\bullet\longleftarrow\bullet.

Now,
Q_{s}(\rho)=\bigcap_{k=1}^{s-1}\pi(\mu,k)
, and moreover
\rho(s)\times Z_{s-1}(\rho)=\bigcap_{k=1}^{s-2}\pi(\mu,k)
, and as before the quotient of the latter by the former is isomorphic to
Z_{s}(\rho)
.
The claimed exact sequence then follows by composing the middle maps in

Z_{s}(\rho)\longrightarrow Z_{s-1}(\rho)\times\rho(s)\longrightarrow Q_{s}(\rho)\longrightarrow\rho(s)\times Z_{s-1}(\rho)\longrightarrow Z_{s}(\rho).\qed

Fix for all
v\in S
an isomorphism
\mathbf{G}_{F_{v}}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\text{GL}_{n/F_{v}}
.
We will assume that
\mathbf{S}_{v}=\mathbf{T}_{v}
correspond to the torus of diagonal matrices, and that
\mathbf{P}_{v}
contains the Borel subgroup of upper-triangular matrices.

Theorem 5.29
.

Let
\pi
be an irreducible smooth representation of
M
over
E
,
U
be as usual and
X\in\mathcal{O}^{\mathfrak{p}_{E}}_{\text{alg}}
.
Then, the spaces
H_{*}(K^{S},U\otimes_{E}\mathcal{F}_{P}^{G}(X,\pi))
and
H^{*}(K^{S},U^{\prime}\otimes_{E}\mathcal{F}_{P}^{G}(X,\pi)^{\prime})
are finite-dimensional.

Proof.
We have proven in
Proposition

5.22
that this holds when
\pi
is the smooth parabolic induction of a supercuspidal representation. As in the proof of
Proposition

5.22
, it’s enough to show this as
\pi
runs through a basis of the Grothendieck group of finite length smooth (admissible) representations of
M
. According to
[
Zel80
, Corollary 7.5]
, one such basis consists of representations of the form

\bigboxtimes_{v\in S}\prod_{i=1}^{r_{v}}Z_{s_{v,i}}(\rho_{v,i})

where for all
v
we have
\sum_{i=1}^{r_{v}}s_{v,i}=n
, and all
s_{v,i}
are positive.
We proceed by induction on
\mathfrak{S}:=\sum_{v\in S}\sum_{i=1}^{r_{v}}(s_{v,i}-1)
.
If
\mathfrak{S}=0
, then we are dealing with a representation of the form
